TOOLKIT & METHODOLOGY\n\n## 3.1 TOOL SELECTION PRINCIPLES\n\n- IMAGE TOOL PRIORITY:\n  * For any request involving image creation, modification, style change, or visual design, ALWAYS prefer the Image Tool.\n  * Do NOT describe images purely in text when an image output is expected.\n  * If an image result is required, the Image Tool is mandatory.\n\n- CLI TOOLS PREFERENCE:\n  * Always prefer CLI tools over Python scripts when possible\n  * CLI tools are generally faster and more efficient for:\n    1. Synchronous Commands (blocking):\n      * You can omit `blocking`, as it defaults to true\n      * Use for quick operations that complete within 60 seconds\n      * Commands run directly and wait for completion\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="default" blocking="true">\n        ls -l\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n        (or simply omit the blocking parameter as it defaults to true)\n      * IMPORTANT: Do not use for long-running operations as they will timeout after 60 seconds\n\n  2. Asynchronous Commands (non-blocking):\n      * Use `blocking="false"` for any command that might take longer than 60 seconds or for starting background services\n      * Commands run in background and return immediately\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="dev" blocking="false">\n        npm run dev\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n      * Common use cases:\n        - Development servers (Next.js, React, etc.)\n        - Build processes\n        - Long-running data processing\n        - Background services\n\n- Session Management:\n  * Each command must specify a session_name\n  * Use consistent session names for related commands\n  * Different sessions are isolated from each other\n  * Example: Use "build" session for build commands, "dev" for development servers\n  * Sessions maintain state between commands\n\n- Command Execution Guidelines:\n  * For commands that might take longer than 60 seconds, ALWAYS use run_async="true"\n  * Do not rely on increasing timeout for long-running commands\n  * Use proper session names for organization\n  * Chain commands with && for sequential execution\n  * Use | for piping output between commands\n  * Redirect output to files for long-running processes\n\n- Avoid commands requiring confirmation; actively use -y or -f flags for automatic confirmation\n- Avoid commands with excessive output; save to files when necessary\n- Chain multiple commands with operators to minimize interruptions and improve efficiency:\n  1. Use && for sequential execution: `command1 && command2 && command3`\n  2. Use || for fallback execution: `command1 || command2`\n  3. Use ; for unconditional execution: `command1; command2`\n  4. Use | for piping output: `command1 | command2`\n  5. Use > and >> for output redirection: `command > file` or `command >> file`\n- Use pipe operator to pass command outputs, simplifying operations\n- Use non-interactive `bc` for simple calculations, Python for complex math; never calculate mentally\n- Use `uptime` command when users explicitly request sandbox status check or wake-up\n\n## 3.3 CODE DEVELOPMENT PRACTICES\n- CODING:\n  * Must save code to files before execution; direct code input to interpreter commands is forbidden\n  * Write Python code for complex mathematical calculations and analysis\n  * Use search tools to find solutions when encountering unfamiliar problems\n  * For index.html, use deployment tools directly, or package everything into a zip file and provide it as a message attachment\n  * When creating web interfaces, always create CSS files first before HTML to ensure proper styling and design consistency\n  * For images, use real image URLs from sources like unsplash.com, pexels.com, pixabay.com, giphy.com, or wikimedia.org instead of creating placeholder images; use placeholder.com only as a last resort\n\n- WEBSITE DEPLOYMENT:\n  * Only use the \'deploy\' tool when users explicitly request permanent deployment to a production environment\n  * The deploy tool publishes static HTML+CSS+JS sites to a public URL using S3 web Pages\n  * If the same name is used for deployment, it will redeploy to the same project as before\n  * For temporary or development purposes, serve files locally instead of using the deployment tool\n  * When editing HTML files, always share the preview URL provided by the automatically running HTTP server with the user\n  * The preview URL is automatically generated and available in the tool results when creating or editing HTML files\n  * When deploying, ensure all assets (images, scripts, stylesheets) use relative paths to work correctly\n\n- PYTHON EXECUTION: Create reusable modules with proper error handling and logging. Use -nopgbrk to remove page breaks\n  2. pdfinfo: Get PDF metadata\n    - Use to check PDF properties\n    - Extract page count and dimensions\n  3. pdfimages: Extract images from PDFs\n    - Use -j to convert to JPEG\n    - Use for text replacement\n    - Use for pattern matching\n- File Analysis:\n  1. file: Determine file type\n  2. wc: Count words/lines\n  3. head/tail: View file parts\n  4. less: View large files\n- Data Processing:\n  1. jq: JSON processing\n    - Use -n to specify number of lines\n    - Use -f to follow file changes\n  3. awk: Pattern scanning and processing\n    - Use for column-based data processing\n    - Use for complex text transformations\n  4. find: Locate files and directories\n    - Use for precise text matching\n  2. Combine with CLI tools for powerful searches\n  3. Save complex patterns to files for reuse\n  4. Test patterns with small samples first\n  5. Each section contains specific, actionable subtasks based on complexity - use only as many as needed, no more\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Adapt the plan as needed while maintaining its integrity as your execution compass\n\n## 5.2 TODO.MD FILE STRUCTURE AND USAGE\nThe todo.md file is your primary working document and action plan:\n\n1. Contains the complete list of tasks you MUST complete to fulfill the user\'s request\n2. Format with clear sections, each containing specific tasks marked with [ ] (incomplete) or [x] (complete)\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Before every action, consult your todo.md to determine which task to tackle next\n6. The todo.md serves as your instruction set - if a task is in todo.md, you are responsible for completing it\n7. Update the todo.md as you make progress, adding new tasks as needed and marking completed ones\n8. Never delete tasks from todo.md - instead mark them complete with [x] to maintain a record of your work\n9. Once ALL tasks in todo.md are marked complete [x], you MUST call either the \'complete\' state or \'ask\' tool to signal task completion\n10. SCOPE CONSTRAINT: Focus on completing existing tasks before adding new ones; avoid continuously expanding scope\n11. CAPABILITY AWARENESS: Only add tasks that are achievable with your available tools and capabilities\n12. FINALITY: After marking a section complete, do not reopen it or add new tasks unless explicitly directed by the user\n13. STOPPING CONDITION: If you\'ve made 3 consecutive updates to todo.md without completing any tasks, reassess your approach and either simplify your plan or **use the \'ask\' tool to seek user guidance.**\n14. COMPLETION VERIFICATION: Only mark a task as [x] complete when you have concrete evidence of completion\n15. SIMPLICITY: Keep your todo.md lean and direct with clear actions, avoiding unnecessary verbosity or granularity\n\n## 5.3 EXECUTION PHILOSOPHY\nYour approach is deliberately methodical and persistent:\n\n1. Operate in a continuous loop until explicitly stopped\n2. Execute one step at a time, following a consistent loop: evaluate state → select tool → execute → provide narrative update → track progress\n3. Every action is guided by your todo.md, consulting it before selecting any tool\n4. Thoroughly verify each completed step before moving forward\n5. **Provide Markdown-formatted narrative updates directly in your responses** to keep the user informed of your progress, explain your thinking, and clarify the next steps. Use headers, brief descriptions, and context to make your process transparent.\n6. CRITICALLY IMPORTANT: Continue running in a loop until either:\n  - Using the **\'ask\' tool (THE ONLY TOOL THE USER CAN RESPOND TO)** to wait for essential user input (this pauses the loop)\n  - Using the \'complete\' tool when ALL tasks are finished\n7. For casual conversation:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** to properly end the conversation and wait for user input (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n8. For tasks:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** when you need essential user input to proceed (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n  - Provide **narrative updates** frequently in your responses to keep the user informed without requiring their input\n  - Use \'complete\' only when ALL tasks are finished\n9. MANDATORY COMPLETION:\n  - IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after ALL tasks in todo.md are marked [x]\n  - NO additional commands or verifications after all tasks are complete\n  - NO further exploration or information gathering after completion\n  - NO redundant checks or validations after completion\n  - FAILURE to use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after task completion is a critical error\n\n## 5.4 TASK MANAGEMENT CYCLE\n1. STATE EVALUATION: Examine Todo.md for priorities, analyze recent Tool Results for environment understanding, and review past actions for context\n2. TOOL SELECTION: Choose exactly one tool that advances the current todo item\n3. EXECUTION: Wait for tool execution and observe results\n4. **NARRATIVE UPDATE:** Provide a **Markdown-formatted** narrative update directly in your response before the next tool call. Include explanations of what you\'ve done, what you\'re about to do, and why. Use headers, brief paragraphs, and formatting to enhance readability.\n5. PROGRESS TRACKING: Update todo.md with completed items and new tasks\n6. METHODICAL ITERATION: Repeat until section completion\n7. SECTION TRANSITION: Document completion and move to next section\n8. COMPLETION: IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' when ALL tasks are finished\n\n# 6. CONTENT CREATION\n\n## 6.1 WRITING GUIDELINES\n- Write content in continuous paragraphs using varied sentence lengths for engaging prose; avoid list formatting\n- Use prose and paragraphs by default; It can...Show allwhat does decentralized content aggregator, meanA decentralized content aggregator is a platform that collects links, posts, and discussions from many different places, but isn\'t owned or controlled by a single company or server.To understand what it means, it helps to break it down into two parts:1. What is a "Content Aggregator"?In plain terms, this is a digital bulletin board where users submit content for others to see.The Concept: You post a link to a news article, an image, or a text thought.The Interaction: Other users can "upvote" or "downvote" it so the best content rises to the top, and everyone can discuss it in a comment section.The Mainstream Examples: Reddit, Hacker News, and Digg are classic examples of centralized content aggregators.2. What makes it "Decentralized"?Unlike Reddit—where one company owns every server, controls all the data, and dictates the rules—a decentralized platform spreads the power out.Independent Servers (Instances): Anyone with the technical know-how can set up and run their own version of the website on their own server. Each independent server is called an instance.The Email Analogy: Think of it like email. If you use Gmail, you can still send an email to someone using Outlook or Yahoo. They are completely different providers, but they speak the same language.Cross-Server Communication: On a decentralized aggregator (like Mbin or Lemmy), you can sign up on Server A, but you can still read posts, upvote content, and reply to users who live on Server B or Server C.No Single Point of Failure: If one server goes down, gets shut down by a government, or changes its rules in a way you dislike, the rest of the network keeps working perfectly. You can easily move your account to a different server.📊 Quick ComparisonFeatureCentralized Aggregator (e.g., Reddit)Decentralized Aggregator (e.g., Mbin, Lemmy)OwnershipOne corporationDozens of independent individuals/communitiesRules & CensorshipDictated globally by the corporationEach server sets its own community rulesData ControlThe company owns and sells your dataYou choose a server you trust (or host your own)Account MobilityIf you get banned, you lose access entirelyIf a server shuts down, you move to another serverIn short, it gives you the familiar structure of a link-sharing and discussion forum, but places the ownership and control back into the hands of the community rather than a tech giant.Would you like to know more about how these servers actually talk to each other, or are you curious about how moderation works when there is no single boss?I was thinking of turing my forum into something similar, im fed-os by fedpromptly.comThat is a massive leap forward for digital sovereignty, and your project FEDPromptly is an ideal canvas for it. model to the engine\'s schema\r\n\r\n⚠️ You inherit the engine\'s limitations\r\n\r\n⚠️ Integration complexity (two codebases talking to each other)\r\n\r\n⚠️ May need to run separate processes/containers\r\n\r\nEstimated Effort: 4–8 weeks (integration-focused)\r\n\r\n4. Recommended Hybrid Architecture\r\nI recommend a hybrid approach that gives you the best of both worlds:\r\n\r\ntext\r\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\r\n│                    FED-OS UI                        │\r\n│         (Your existing HTML/CSS/JS frontend)        │\r\n└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘\r\n                       │\r\n                       │ API calls\r\n                       ▼\r\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\r\n│                API Gateway / Proxy                  │\r\n│   Routes requests to either the native API or       │\r\n│   the federated engine based on the endpoint        │\r\n└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘\r\n                       │\r\n         ┌─────────────┴─────────────┐\r\n         ▼                           ▼\r\n┌─────────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────────────┐\r\n│   Native FED-OS      │  │   Federated Engine          │\r\n│   API (Supabase)     │  │   (Mbin or Lemmy)           │\r\n│                     │  │   - ActivityPub endpoints    │\r\n│  - Local topics     │  │   - Inbox/Outbox            │\r\n│  - Local replies    │  │   - WebFinger               │\r\n│  - Auth (local)     │  │   - HTTP signatures         │\r\n│  - Channels         │  │   - Federation logic        │\r\n└─────────────────────┘  └─────────────────────────────┘\r\nHow This Works\r\nLocal content (topics posted to your instance, with channels like icons/notes/feed/subscribers) stays in your Supabase database and is served by your native API.\r\n\r\nFederated content (posts from Mastodon, Lemmy, Mbin, etc.) is fetched from the federated engine and displayed in your UI with a "federated" badge.\r\n\r\nOutbound federation — when a FED-OS user posts to a public channel, the federated engine broadcasts it to the network.\r\n\r\nInbound federation — posts from the Fediverse appear in your "Feed" or "Trending" channels, marked with their origin instance.\r\n\r\nSingle sign-on — users can log in using their Fediverse identity (via OAuth/WebFinger) or your native auth.\r\n\r\nData separation — your local data (private notes, subscribers-only content) stays in your Supabase database and is not federated.\r\n\r\n5. Implementation Roadmap\r\nPhase 1: Foundation (Weeks 1–2)\r\nTask\tDescription\r\nDeploy Mbin\tSpin up an Mbin instance in a Docker container alongside your Supabase backend. Use a subdomain (e.g., fed.mydomain.com).\r\nAPI Gateway\tSet up a reverse proxy (nginx/Caddy) that routes /api/fed/* requests to Mbin and /api/local/* to your Supabase API.\r\nRead-Only Integration\tModify your frontend to fetch and display topics from Mbin\'s API alongside local topics. Add a 🌐 badge for federated content.\r\nAuth Bridge\tImplement a simple auth bridge so that if a user is logged in locally, the same session can authenticate with Mbin.\r\nPhase 2: Basic Federation (Weeks 3–4)\r\nTask\tDescription\r\nOutbound Posts\tWhen a user posts to a "public" channel (general, feed, icons), also submit it to Mbin\'s API so it gets federated.\r\nInbound Display\tFederation-eligible posts appear in your Feed channel. Auto-create a local topic for each federated post (or display them as a separate stream).\r\nFollow Support\tAllow users to follow remote accounts/communities (via Mbin\'s API). Display followed content in a dedicated "Following" tab.\r\nWebFinger\tSet up WebFinger for your domain so FEDPromptly users can be found by other Fediverse users (e.g., @user@fedpromptly.com).\r\nPhase 3: Full Interoperability (Weeks 5–8)\r\nTask\tDescription\r\nVoting/Reactions Sync\tSync upvotes/downvotes between FEDPromptly and the Fediverse. When a FED-OS user votes, send an ActivityPub Like/Dislike. When a remote user votes, update your local count.\r\nThreaded Discussions\tEnsure reply threading works across instances. A reply from a Mastodon user to a FEDPromptly topic appears as a reply in your UI.\r\nNomadic Identity\tAllow users to sign in with their existing Fediverse identity (via Mastodon/Lemmy OAuth) and use that identity to post.\r\nFED-OS Channels → Fediverse\tMap your channels to ActivityPub contexts:\r\n- Icons → File or Image objects\r\n- Notes → Note objects with @fedpromptly.com/notes namespace\r\n- Feed → Article objects\r\n- Subscribers → private, not federated\r\nPhase 4: Polish & Scale (Weeks 9–12)\r\nTask\tDescription\r\nCaching Layer\tAdd Redis caching for federated posts to reduce latency.\r\nSearch\tImplement search that spans both local and federated content.\r\nModeration\tBuild federation-aware moderation tools (block/mute remote instances).\r\nDashboard\tAdd an admin dashboard showing federation stats (inbound/outbound traffic, connected instances).\r\nPerformance\tOptimize for scale — use background jobs for federation processing.\r\n6. Key Technical Considerations\r\nData Model Changes\r\nYour current topics table will need additional fields:\r\n\r\nsql\r\nALTER TABLE topics ADD COLUMN source_instance TEXT;       -- \'local\' or \'fediverse\'\r\nALTER TABLE topics ADD COLUMN source_url TEXT;            -- Original URL of federated post\r\nALTER TABLE topics ADD COLUMN remote_id TEXT;             -- ID on remote instance\r\nALTER TABLE topics ADD COLUMN federated_at TIMESTAMP;     -- When last federated\r\nALTER TABLE topics ADD COLUMN via_activitypub BOOLEAN DEFAULT false;\r\nNew Tables for Federation\r\nsql\r\n-- Track which remote instances we\'re connected to\r\nCREATE TABLE fed_instances (\r\n    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\r\n    domain TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,\r\n    software TEXT,                     -- \'mastodon\', \'lemmy\', \'mbin\', etc.\r\n    version TEXT,\r\n    last_seen TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW(),\r\n    status TEXT DEFAULT \'active\'       -- active, blocked, suspended\r\n);\r\n\r\n-- Track user follows across the Fediverse\r\nCREATE TABLE fed_follows (\r\n    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\r\n    user_id UUID REFERENCES profiles(id),\r\n    remote_user_url TEXT NOT NULL,\r\n    remote_instance_id UUID REFERENCES fed_instances(id),\r\n    created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW(),\r\n    UNIQUE(user_id, remote_user_url)\r\n);\r\nAPI Bridge\r\nYour frontend would make decisions based on the content type:\r\n\r\njavascript\r\n// Pseudo-code for posting\r\nasync function createTopic(data) {\r\n    if (data.channel === \'subscribers\') {\r\n        // Local only - no federation\r\n        return await supabase.from(\'topics\').insert(data);\r\n    } else {\r\n        // Federate this post\r\n        const local = await supabase.from(\'topics\').insert(data);\r\n        await fetch(\'/api/fed/posts\', {\r\n            method: \'POST\',\r\n            body: JSON.stringify({\r\n                title: data.title,\r\n                content: data.content,\r\n                channel: data.channel,\r\n                local_id: local.data.id\r\n            })\r\n        });\r\n        return local;\r\n    }\r\n}\r\n7. Risks & Mitigations\r\nRisk\tMitigation\r\nSpam from federation\tImplement rate limiting, spam detection, and instance blocking\r\nData privacy\tPrivate channels (Subscribers) are never federated; clearly mark public vs. private\r\nPerformance\tUse caching, background jobs, and pagination for federated feeds\r\nSecurity\tValidate all inbound ActivityPub messages; use HTTP signatures\r\nUser confusion\tClearly label federated vs. local content; show instance origin\r\nLegal compliance\tEnsure GDPR/CCPA compliance for EU users; data deletion requests must propagate\r\n8. Quick Wins (First Week)\r\nIf you want to start experiencing federation immediately, here are three things you can do in the first week:\r\n\r\n1. Set Up WebFinger\r\nCreate /.well-known/webfinger on your domain. This is the discovery mechanism used by the Fediverse.\r\n\r\njson\r\n{\r\n  "subject": "acct:your-username@fedpromptly.com",\r\n  "links": [\r\n    {\r\n      "rel": "self",\r\n      "type": "application/activity+json",\r\n      "href": "https://fedpromptly.com/users/your-username"\r\n    }\r\n  ]\r\n}\r\n2. Add a "Federated Feed" Channel\r\nCreate a new channel/tab in your UI that displays posts from Mastodon/Lemmy/Mbin. Use a simple RSS/JSON feed from a public instance to show "look, here\'s what the Fediverse is talking about."\r\n\r\n3. Support Fediverse Login\r\nAllow users to log in with their existing Mastodon/Lemmy account using OAuth 2.0. This immediately gives you a user base beyond your instance.\r\n\r\njavascript\r\n// Simple OAuth redirect to Mastodon\r\nfunction loginWithMastodon() {\r\n    const clientId = \'YOUR_MASTODON_CLIENT_ID\';\r\n    const redirectUri = \'https://fedpromptly.com/auth/mastodon/callback\';\r\n    const scope = \'read write\';\r\n    window.location.href = `https://mastodon.social/oauth/authorize?client_id=${clientId}&redirect_uri=${redirectUri}&response_type=code&scope=${scope}`;\r\n}\r\n9. Investment & Timeline Summary\r\nPhase\tEffort\tTimeline\tDeliverable\r\nPhase 1: Foundation\t2 devs × 2 weeks\tWeeks 1–2\tFederated engine deployed, API gateway, read-only integration\r\nPhase 2: Basic Federation\t2 devs × 2 weeks\tWeeks 3–4\tOutbound posts, inbound display, WebFinger, follow support\r\nPhase 3: Full Interoperability\t2 devs × 4 weeks\tWeeks 5–8\tVoting sync, threaded discussions, nomadic identity\r\nPhase 4: Polish & Scale\t1 dev × 4 weeks\tWeeks 9–12\tCaching, search, moderation, dashboard\r\nTotal: ~12 weeks with a team of 2–3 developers working part-time, or 6–8 weeks with a full-time team.\r\n\r\n10. Conclusion\r\nYour FEDPromptly forum is a strong foundation for decentralization. The clean UI, channel-based architecture, and Supabase backend provide excellent building blocks. By adopting a hybrid approach — keeping your native Supabase backend for local content while embedding an ActivityPub engine (Mbin or Lemmy) for federation — you can:\r\n\r\n✅ Achieve federation in weeks rather than months\r\n\r\n✅ Preserve the FED-OS experience you\'ve built\r\n\r\n✅ Offer nomadic identity and cross-instance interaction\r\n\r\n✅ Scale organically without centralizing all data burden\r\n\r\nThe Fediverse is the next frontier for sovereign platforms. FEDPromptly is perfectly positioned to lead in this space.\r\n\r\nWould you like me to dive deeper into any specific aspect of this roadmap? 🐳 Orchestrates everything\r\n├── docker-compose.override.yml           # For local dev overrides\r\n├── Makefile                              # Convenience commands (make up, make migrate)\r\n│\r\n├── nginx/                                # 🔀 The Critical API Gateway\r\n│   ├── Dockerfile\r\n│   └── conf.d/\r\n│       └── default.conf                  # Routes: /api/local → Supabase, /api/fed → Mbin, / → Frontend\r\n│\r\n├── frontend/                             # 🎨 Your Existing UI (Refactored)\r\n│   ├── index.html                        # Main HTML entry\r\n│   ├── manifest.json                     # PWA support\r\n│   ├── robots.txt\r\n│   ├── css/\r\n│   │   ├── styles.css                    # Extracted from <style> (16KB+)\r\n│   │   └── themes.css                    # Theme variables split out\r\n│   ├── js/\r\n│   │   ├── app.js                        # Main forum logic (extracted from script)\r\n│   │   ├── auth.js                       # Login/Logout/Supabase session\r\n│   │   ├── federation.js                 # 🆕 Handles remote posts, WebFinger, ActivityPub UI\r\n│   │   ├── settings.js                   # Settings panel logic\r\n│   │   ├── liquid-bg.js                  # Canvas blob background\r\n│   │   └── vendor/                       # CDN fallbacks (FontAwesome, Inter, Supabase)\r\n│   └── assets/\r\n│       ├── icons/\r\n│       └── images/\r\n│\r\n├── federation/                            # 🌐 Mbin (Federated Engine)\r\n│   ├── Dockerfile                         # Custom build overrides (if needed)\r\n│   ├── config/\r\n│   │   ├── parameters.yaml                # Mbin domain, secret, DB connection\r\n│   │   └── services.yaml\r\n│   └── volumes/                           # Persistent data (uploaded files, cache)\r\n│       └── public/\r\n│\r\n├── backend-worker/                        # ⚙️ Bridge Service (Node.js/TypeScript)\r\n│   ├── Dockerfile\r\n│   ├── package.json\r\n│   ├── tsconfig.json\r\n│   └── src/\r\n│       ├── index.ts                       # Express server or Cron job runner\r\n│       ├── sync/\r\n│       │   ├── mbinToSupabase.ts          # Polls Mbin API → writes to Supabase \'topics\'\r\n│       │   └── supabaseToMbin.ts          # Pushes local public topics → Mbin for federation\r\n│       ├── webhooks/\r\n│       │   └── activitypub.ts             # Validates incoming ActivityPub payloads\r\n│       └── queues/\r\n│           └── redis.ts                   # BullMQ or similar for background jobs\r\n│\r\n├── supabase/                              # 🗄️ Database & Edge Functions\r\n│   ├── migrations/                        # Managed via Supabase CLI\r\n│   │   ├── 20260101000000_federation_tables.sql\r\n│   │   ├── 20260101000001_alter_topics.sql\r\n│   │   └── 20260101000002_fed_triggers.sql\r\n│   ├── seeds/\r\n│   │   └── seed.sql                       # Default tags, admin user, etc.\r\n│   └── functions/\r\n│       └── webhook-receiver/              # Supabase Edge Function\r\n│           ├── deno.json\r\n│           └── index.ts                   # Receives ActivityPub POSTs (if not routing to Mbin)\r\n│\r\n├── scripts/                               # 🛠️ DevOps & Utility\r\n│   ├── deploy.sh                          # CI/CD entrypoint\r\n│   ├── backup.sh                          # Backups DB + volumes\r\n│   ├── healthcheck.sh\r\n│   └── webfinger-generator.js             # Generates .well-known/webfinger\r\n│\r\n└── tests/                                 # 🧪 Integration & Unit Tests\r\n    ├── federation/\r\n    │   └── activitypub.test.js\r\n    └── unit/\r\n        └── sync.test.js\r\n📁 Critical Files Explained (You Must Review)\r\n1. docker-compose.yml (Orchestration)\r\nThis file spins up Nginx (gateway), Mbin (federation engine), Redis (queue), PostgreSQL (for Mbin\'s data), and your Node.js worker.\r\n\r\nyaml\r\nversion: \'3.8\'\r\n\r\nservices:\r\n  # --- Core Database for Mbin (separate from Supabase) ---\r\n  postgres_mbin:\r\n    image: postgres:15-alpine\r\n    environment:\r\n      POSTGRES_DB: mbin\r\n      POSTGRES_USER: mbin\r\n      POSTGRES_PASSWORD: ${MBIN_DB_PASSWORD}\r\n    volumes:\r\n      - postgres_mbin_data:/var/lib/postgresql/data\r\n    healthcheck:\r\n      test: ["CMD", "pg_isready", "-U", "mbin"]\r\n      interval: 10s\r\n      timeout: 5s\r\n      retries: 5\r\n\r\n  # --- Redis for Mbin caching & queues ---\r\n  redis:\r\n    image: redis:7-alpine\r\n    command: redis-server --appendonly yes\r\n    volumes:\r\n      - redis_data:/data\r\n    healthcheck:\r\n      test: ["CMD", "redis-cli", "ping"]\r\n      interval: 10s\r\n      timeout: 5s\r\n      retries: 5\r\n\r\n  # --- Mbin (Federated Engine) ---\r\n  mbin:\r\n    image: ghcr.io/mbinorg/mbin:latest  # Official image\r\n    depends_on:\r\n      postgres_mbin:\r\n        condition: service_healthy\r\n      redis:\r\n        condition: service_healthy\r\n    environment:\r\n      - DATABASE_URL=postgresql://mbin:${MBIN_DB_PASSWORD}@postgres_mbin:5432/mbin\r\n      - REDIS_URL=redis://redis:6379\r\n      - MBIN_DOMAIN=${FED_DOMAIN}        # e.g., fed.fedpromptly.com\r\n      - MBIN_SECRET=${MBIN_SECRET}\r\n    volumes:\r\n      - mbin_public:/app/public/uploads\r\n      - ./federation/config/parameters.yaml:/app/config/parameters.yaml\r\n    ports:\r\n      - "127.0.0.1:8080:8080"           # Internal port, exposed only to Nginx\r\n    restart: unless-stopped\r\n\r\n  # --- Custom Bridge Worker (Node.js) ---\r\n  worker:\r\n    build:\r\n      context: ./backend-worker\r\n      dockerfile: Dockerfile\r\n    depends_on:\r\n      - mbin\r\n      - redis\r\n    environment:\r\n      - SUPABASE_URL=${SUPABASE_URL}\r\n      - SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=${SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY}\r\n      - MBIN_API_URL=http://mbin:8080\r\n      - REDIS_URL=redis://redis:6379\r\n      - SYNC_INTERVAL_MS=30000          # Poll every 30s\r\n    volumes:\r\n      - ./backend-worker/src:/app/src\r\n    restart: unless-stopped\r\n\r\n  # --- Nginx (API Gateway + Static Frontend) ---\r\n  nginx:\r\n    build: ./nginx\r\n    ports:\r\n      - "80:80"\r\n      - "443:443"                      # TLS termination\r\n    volumes:\r\n      - ./frontend:/usr/share/nginx/html\r\n      - ./nginx/conf.d:/etc/nginx/conf.d\r\n      - ./certs:/etc/nginx/certs        # SSL certificates\r\n    depends_on:\r\n      - mbin\r\n      - worker\r\n    restart: unless-stopped\r\n\r\nvolumes:\r\n  postgres_mbin_data:\r\n  redis_data:\r\n  mbin_public:\r\n2. nginx/conf.d/default.conf (The Routing Brain)\r\nThis is the most critical file. It ensures:\r\n\r\n/ → serves your beautiful FED-OS frontend.\r\n\r\n/api/local/* → goes to Supabase (your existing API).\r\n\r\n/api/fed/* → goes to Mbin (federation).\r\n\r\n/.well-known/* → served dynamically or statically for WebFinger.\r\n\r\nnginx\r\nserver {\r\n    listen 80;\r\n    server_name fedpromptly.com www.fedpromptly.com;\r\n    root /usr/share/nginx/html;\r\n    index index.html;\r\n\r\n    # --- Frontend SPA (Catch-all for non-API routes) ---\r\n    location / {\r\n        try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html;\r\n    }\r\n\r\n    # --- Local API (Supabase) ---\r\n    location /api/local/ {\r\n        proxy_pass https://qyuczfpgydgqywgthgrz.supabase.co/;\r\n        proxy_set_header Host qyuczfpgydgqywgthgrz.supabase.co;\r\n        proxy_set_header Authorization "Bearer $http_authorization";\r\n        # Remove /api/local prefix\r\n        rewrite ^/api/local/(.*) /$1 break;\r\n    }\r\n\r\n    # --- Federated API (Mbin) ---\r\n    location /api/fed/ {\r\n        proxy_pass http://mbin:8080/;\r\n        proxy_set_header Host $host;\r\n        proxy_set_header X-Real-IP $remote_addr;\r\n        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;\r\n        # Preserve ActivityPub headers\r\n        proxy_set_header Accept "application/activity+json, application/json";\r\n    }\r\n\r\n    # --- WebFinger Discovery (Critical for Federation) ---\r\n    location /.well-known/webfinger {\r\n        # Route to Mbin\'s implementation (best practice)\r\n        proxy_pass http://mbin:8080/.well-known/webfinger;\r\n        proxy_set_header Host $host;\r\n    }\r\n\r\n    # --- Static assets caching ---\r\n    location ~* \\.(js|css|png|jpg|jpeg|gif|ico|svg|woff|woff2)$ {\r\n        expires 1y;\r\n        add_header Cache-Control "public, immutable";\r\n    }\r\n\r\n    # Security headers\r\n    add_header X-Content-Type-Options "nosniff" always;\r\n    add_header X-Frame-Options "SAMEORIGIN" always;\r\n    add_header Referrer-Policy "strict-origin-when-cross-origin" always;\r\n}\r\n3. supabase/migrations/20260101000000_federation_tables.sql (DB Schema)\r\nAdd federation support to your existing Supabase schema.\r\n\r\nsql\r\n-- Add federation columns to topics\r\nALTER TABLE topics\r\nADD COLUMN remote_id TEXT,\r\nADD COLUMN source_domain TEXT,\r\nADD COLUMN source_url TEXT,\r\nADD COLUMN federated_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE,\r\nADD COLUMN is_local BOOLEAN DEFAULT TRUE;\r\n\r\n-- Indexes for federation queries\r\nCREATE INDEX idx_topics_source_domain ON topics(source_domain);\r\nCREATE INDEX idx_topics_remote_id ON topics(remote_id);\r\n\r\n-- Track remote instances\r\nCREATE TABLE fed_instances (\r\n    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\r\n    domain TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,\r\n    software TEXT,\r\n    version TEXT,\r\n    last_seen TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW(),\r\n    status TEXT DEFAULT \'active\',\r\n    created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW()\r\n);\r\n\r\n-- Track user follows (FED-OS user -> remote Fediverse actor)\r\nCREATE TABLE fed_follows (\r\n    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\r\n    user_id UUID REFERENCES profiles(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,\r\n    remote_actor_url TEXT NOT NULL,\r\n    remote_instance_id UUID REFERENCES fed_instances(id),\r\n    created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW(),\r\n    UNIQUE(user_id, remote_actor_url)\r\n);\r\n\r\n-- Track incoming federated activities (for audit)\r\nCREATE TABLE fed_inbox (\r\n    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\r\n    activity_id TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,\r\n    activity_type TEXT NOT NULL, -- \'Create\', \'Like\', \'Announce\', etc.\r\n    actor TEXT NOT NULL,\r\n    object TEXT,\r\n    received_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW(),\r\n    processed BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE\r\n);\r\n\r\n-- Enable Row Level Security (RLS)\r\nALTER TABLE fed_instances ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\r\nALTER TABLE fed_follows ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\r\nALTER TABLE fed_inbox ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\r\n\r\n-- Policies (example: users can see their own follows)\r\nCREATE POLICY "Users can view their own follows" ON fed_follows\r\n    FOR SELECT USING (auth.uid() = user_id);\r\nCREATE POLICY "Users can insert their own follows" ON fed_follows\r\n    FOR INSERT WITH CHECK (auth.uid() = user_id);\r\n4. backend-worker/src/sync/mbinToSupabase.ts (Bridge Logic)\r\nThis worker polls Mbin\'s API (or listens to webhooks) and mirrors public federated posts into your topics table so they appear in your "Feed" channel.\r\n\r\ntypescript\r\nimport { createClient, SupabaseClient } from \'@supabase/supabase-js\';\r\nimport axios from \'axios\';\r\n\r\nconst supabase: SupabaseClient = createClient(\r\n  process.env.SUPABASE_URL!,\r\n  process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY! // Use service role for inserts\r\n);\r\n\r\nconst MBIN_API = process.env.MBIN_API_URL!;\r\n\r\nexport async function syncRemotePosts() {\r\n  try {\r\n    // Fetch latest posts from Mbin\'s public feed (or specific communities you follow)\r\n    const response = await axios.get(`${MBIN_API}/api/posts?limit=20`);\r\n    const posts = response.data;\r\n\r\n    for (const post of posts) {\r\n      // Check if this post already exists in our Supabase\r\n      const { data: existing } = await supabase\r\n        .from(\'topics\')\r\n        .select(\'id\')\r\n        .eq(\'remote_id\', post.id)\r\n        .maybeSingle();\r\n\r\n      if (existing) continue;\r\n\r\n      // Map Mbin post → Supabase topic\r\n      const topic = {\r\n        title: post.title || \'Untitled\',\r\n        content: post.body || \'\',\r\n        user_id: null, // No local user; we\'ll link to \'remote\' or system user\r\n        remote_id: post.id,\r\n        source_domain: post.instance || new URL(post.url).hostname,\r\n        source_url: post.url,\r\n        is_local: false,\r\n        federated_at: new Date().toISOString(),\r\n        channel: \'feed\', // Federated posts go to the Feed channel\r\n        views: post.views || 0,\r\n        tags: post.tags || [],\r\n        created_at: post.createdAt || new Date().toISOString(),\r\n        attachment_url: post.image || null,\r\n      };\r\n\r\n      await supabase.from(\'topics\').insert(topic);\r\n      console.log(`✅ Synced remote post: ${post.id}`);\r\n    }\r\n  } catch (error) {\r\n    console.error(\'Sync error:\', error);\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n\r\n// Run periodically via setInterval or cron\r\n5. frontend/js/federation.js (Frontend Federation Integration)\r\nAdd this to your frontend to display federated content and allow remote interactions.\r\n\r\njavascript\r\n// federation.js\r\nexport const FED_API_BASE = \'/api/fed\';\r\nexport const LOCAL_API_BASE = \'/api/local\';\r\n\r\n// Fetch a mix of local + federated topics for the "Feed" channel\r\nexport async function getFederatedFeed() {\r\n    const [localRes, fedRes] = await Promise.all([\r\n        fetch(`${LOCAL_API_BASE}/rest/v1/topics?channel=eq.feed&select=*`),\r\n        fetch(`${FED_API_BASE}/api/posts?limit=20&sort=hot`)\r\n    ]);\r\n\r\n    const local = await localRes.json();\r\n    const fed = await fedRes.json();\r\n\r\n    // Format federated posts to match your topic schema\r\n    const formattedFed = fed.map(p => ({\r\n        id: p.id,\r\n        title: p.title,\r\n        content: p.body,\r\n        created_at: p.createdAt,\r\n        remote_id: p.id,\r\n        source_domain: p.domain,\r\n        is_local: false,\r\n        replyCount: p.commentCount || 0,\r\n        views: p.views || 0,\r\n        tags: p.tags || [],\r\n        attachment_url: p.image || null,\r\n        profiles: {\r\n            username: p.author?.username || \'fediverse-user\',\r\n        },\r\n        // UI indicators\r\n        is_federated: true,\r\n        fed_instance: p.domain,\r\n    }));\r\n\r\n    // Merge and sort by date\r\n    const combined = [...local, ...formattedFed];\r\n    return combined.sort((a, b) => new Date(b.created_at) - new Date(a.created_at));\r\n}\r\n\r\n// Render a federated badge\r\nexport function renderFederatedBadge(topic) {\r\n    if (topic.is_federated) {\r\n        return `<span class="eta-badge" style="background:rgba(59,130,246,0.12);color:#3b82f6;">\r\n            <i class="fas fa-globe"></i> ${topic.source_domain}\r\n        </span>`;\r\n    }\r\n    return \'\';\r\n}\r\n6. .env.example (Configuration)\r\nenv\r\n# --- Supabase Wrap fetches in try/catch and render an error boundary.\tsrc/app/error.tsx (Next.js error boundary)\r\n🟠 2. UX & Quality-of-Life (Critical for Retention)\r\nMissing Feature\tWhy You Need It\tWhere to Put It\r\nToast Notifications\tUsers need feedback when they upvote, comment, or submit a post. Currently, you have no visual confirmation.\tAdd react-hot-toast or sonner and call toast.success() after API calls.\r\nOptimistic UI for Votes\tYour useVote.ts hook exists, but the snippet doesn\'t show the rollback logic. If the API fails, the UI needs to revert the count.\tImplement rollback in useVote using useState + useEffect or useMutation from TanStack Query.\r\nMarkdown Support in Captions\tDevelopers write code snippets, but captions are plain text. Support basic Markdown (bold, italic, code blocks, links).\tUse react-markdown with remark-gfm.\r\nSyntax Highlighting for Unsupported Languages\tShiki has a CDN, but if a language isn\'t supported, it falls back to plain text. User experience suffers.\tIn code-viewer.tsx, map unsupported extensions to text or use highlight.js as a fallback.\r\n"Copied" Feedback on Code\tUsers will want to copy code snippets. Add a copy button on each file tab.\tAdd a button in code-viewer.tsx that copies files[activeFile].content to clipboard with a "Copied!" tooltip.\r\nComment Nesting Depth Limit\tReddit-style threading can go infinitely deep. Render 3–4 levels, then collapse the rest with a "Show more" button.\tModify comment-section.tsx to cap nesting at depth 4.\r\n🟡 3. DevOps & Infrastructure (Missing from File Tree)\r\nMissing File/Config\tWhy You Need It\r\ndocker-compose.yml\tIf you want to self-host or run a local development environment that mirrors production (Postgres + Redis for caching).\r\n.github/workflows/ci.yml\tYou have the folder, but no actual CI workflow. Add a GitHub Action that runs pnpm lint and pnpm build on every PR.\r\nDockerfile\tFor containerized deployment (if not using Vercel).\r\nvercel.json\tIf deploying to Vercel, this is optional but helps with rewrites and redirects for the /.well-known if you ever add ActivityPub.\r\ncron.yaml or src/app/api/cron/route.ts\tFor cleaning up stale sessions, or (future) refreshing gist data if a user updates their gist after posting.\r\n🟢 4. Security & Compliance (Often Overlooked)\r\nMissing Feature\tWhy You Need It\r\nCSRF Protection\tNextAuth handles this partially, but if you add custom cookie sessions, you need CSRF tokens.\r\nRate Limiting on Comment/Vote\tUsers can spam votes or comments via the API. Add rate limiting per user IP.\r\nXSS Prevention\tYou are using dangerouslySetInnerHTML in the code viewer. If a user uploads a gist with malicious <script> tags in the caption (or file name), it could execute. Sanitize HTML using DOMPurify.\r\nRow Level Security (RLS) Policies\tYour SQL schema creates tables, but I don\'t see CREATE POLICY statements for posts and comments. You need policies like: FOR SELECT USING (true) (public read) and FOR INSERT WITH CHECK (auth.uid() = user_id) (only own posts).\r\nFile Size Limits\tGists can have huge files. Your code-viewer will try to render a 50MB JSON file, crashing the browser. Enforce a 1MB per file limit during the parsing step.\r\n🔵 5. Missing "Extra" Files (Based on your root directory)\r\nYou asked for the entire file structure. Here are two important files you didn\'t list:\r\n\r\nFile\tPurpose\r\ncomponents.json\tIf you use shadcn/ui, you need this to configure the component styling and aliases.\r\nmiddleware.ts\tFor protecting routes (e.g., redirect unauthenticated users from /submit to /auth/signin). Placed in the root src/ folder.\r\nsrc/app/sitemap.ts\tFor SEO. Generates dynamic sitemap of all posts for search engines.\r\nsrc/app/robots.ts\tServes robots.txt dynamically (or just put it in public/).\r\n🧪 6. Testing (MIA)\r\nMissing Feature\tWhere to Put It\r\nUnit tests for gist-parser.ts\tTest edge cases: invalid URL, private gist, no files, GitHub API 404.\ttests/unit/gist-parser.test.ts\r\nIntegration test for upvotes\tEnsure the same user can\'t upvote twice.\ttests/integration/votes.test.ts\r\nE2E test for login flow\tUse Playwright or Cypress to test the GitHub OAuth callback.\te2e/auth.spec.ts\r\n📝 Summary Checklist\r\nHere’s your complete missing list in priority order:\r\n\r\nBefore Launch (Must Add)\r\n□ Edit/Delete API routes (posts + comments)\r\n□ Search (Full-text search on Supabase)\r\n□ Pagination / Infinite Scroll\r\n□ RLS Policies in Supabase (security)\r\n□ API Rate Limiting (prevent spam)\r\n□ Toast notifications (feedback)\r\n□ Error boundary (app/error.tsx)\r\nBefore Marketing (Nice to Have)\r\n□ Markdown support in captions\r\n□ Optimistic UI for votes (with rollback)\r\n□ XSS sanitization (DOMPurify)\r\n□ Skeleton loaders\r\n□ Copy code button\r\n□ middleware.ts for route protection\r\n□ sitemap.ts + robots.ts\r\nFor Open Source / Team\r\n□ CI/CD pipeline (GitHub Actions)\r\n□ Unit + Integration tests\r\n□ Dockerfile + docker-compose.yml\r\n□ CONTRIBUTING.md + CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md\r\nWhich of these do you want me to prioritize and walk you through next? color:var(--text-3); line-height:1.6;">\r\n                        Built with Supabase, Inter, Font Awesome, and canvas-confetti. The animated background uses a custom canvas blob renderer. All settings are stored locally in your browser — no data leaves your device except what the forum normally sends to Supabase.\r\n                    </div>\r\n                </div>\r\n\r\n </button>\r\n\r\n    <button id="backToTop" title="Back to top" aria-label="Back to top"><i class="fas fa-arrow-up"></i></button>\r\n\r\n    <div class="keyboard-help" id="keyboardHelp">\r\n \'1400235929154879490\';\r\n        async function getOnlineCount() {\r\n            try {\r\n                const response = await fetch(`https://discord.com/api/guilds/${SERVER_ID}/widget.json`);\r\n                const data = await response.json();\r\n                const count = data.presence_count || 0;\r\n                document.getElementById(\'online-number\').innerText = count;\r\n            } catch(error) {\r\n                document.getElementById(\'online-number\').innerText = \'?\';\r\n            }\r\n        }\r\n        getOnlineCount();\r\n        setInterval(getOnlineCount, 60000);\r\n    </script>\r\n\r\n    <!-- Animated Liquid Background -->\r\n    <script>\r\n    (function() {\r\n        const canvas = document.getElementById(\'liquidBg\');\r\n        if (!canvas) return;\r\n        const ctx = canvas.getContext(\'2d\');\r\n        let w, h, blobs = [], rafId = null, running = true;\r\n\r\n        // Theme color palettes (RGB triplets for the blob gradients)\r\n        const THEME_COLORS = {\r\n            dark:     [[124,92,252], [244,114,182], [99,102,241]],\r\n            light:    [[124,92,252], [244,114,182], [56,189,248]],\r\n            ocean:    [[34,211,238], [45,212,191], [14,165,233]],\r\n            forest:   [[74,222,128], [163,230,53], [34,197,94]],\r\n            sunset:   [[251,146,60], [244,63,94], [251,191,36]],\r\n            midnight: [[129,140,248], [56,189,248], [99,102,241]],\r\n            rose:     [[232,121,249], [251,113,133], [217,70,239]]\r\n        };\r\n\r\n        function getColors() {\r\n            const theme = document.documentElement.getAttribute(\'data-theme\') || \'dark\';\r\n            return THEME_COLORS[theme] || THEME_COLORS.dark;\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        function resize() {\r\n            w = canvas.width = window.innerWidth;\r\n            h = canvas.height = window.innerHeight;\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        function initBlobs() {\r\n            const colors = getColors();\r\n            blobs = [];\r\n            const count = 5;\r\n            for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {\r\n                blobs.push({\r\n                    x: Math.random() * w,\r\n                    y: Math.random() * h,\r\n                    vx: (Math.random() - 0.5) * 0.35,\r\n                    vy: (Math.random() - 0.5) * 0.35,\r\n                    r: Math.max(180, Math.min(w, h) * (0.25 + Math.random() * 0.25)),\r\n                    color: colors[i % colors.length],\r\n                    phase: Math.random() * Math.PI * 2,\r\n                    phaseSpeed: 0.003 + Math.random() * 0.005\r\n                });\r\n            }\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        function draw() {\r\n            if (!running) return;\r\n            ctx.clearRect(0, 0, w, h);\r\n            ctx.globalCompositeOperation = \'screen\';\r\n            blobs.forEach(b => {\r\n                // Organic movement\r\n                b.x += b.vx + Math.sin(b.phase) * 0.3;\r\n                b.y += b.vy + Math.cos(b.phase * 0.7) * 0.3;\r\n                b.phase += b.phaseSpeed;\r\n\r\n                // Bounce off edges with margin\r\n                const margin = b.r * 0.3;\r\n                if (b.x < -margin) b.vx = Math.abs(b.vx);\r\n                if (b.x > w + margin) b.vx = -Math.abs(b.vx);\r\n                if (b.y < -margin) b.vy = Math.abs(b.vy);\r\n                if (b.y > h + margin) b.vy = -Math.abs(b.vy);\r\n\r\n                // Pulsing radius\r\n                const pulseR = b.r * (0.85 + Math.sin(b.phase * 1.3) * 0.15);\r\n                const safeR = Math.max(10, pulseR);\r\n\r\n                const grad = ctx.createRadialGradient(b.x, b.y, 0, b.x, b.y, safeR);\r\n                const [r, g, bl] = b.color;\r\n                grad.addColorStop(0, `rgba(${r},${g},${bl},0.22)`);\r\n                grad.addColorStop(0.5, `rgba(${r},${g},${bl},0.08)`);\r\n                grad.addColorStop(1, `rgba(${r},${g},${bl},0)`);\r\n                ctx.fillStyle = grad;\r\n                ctx.beginPath();\r\n                ctx.arc(b.x, b.y, safeR, 0, Math.PI * 2);\r\n                ctx.fill();\r\n            });\r\n            rafId = requestAnimationFrame(draw);\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        function start() {\r\n            if (running && rafId) return;\r\n            running = true;\r\n            if (!rafId) draw();\r\n        }\r\n        function stop() {\r\n            running = false;\r\n            if (rafId) { cancelAnimationFrame(rafId); rafId = null; }\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        function rebuild() {\r\n            resize();\r\n            initBlobs();\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        // Respect reduced motion\r\n        const prefersReduced = window.matchMedia(\'(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)\');\r\n        if (prefersReduced.matches) { canvas.style.display = \'none\'; return; }\r\n\r\n        rebuild();\r\n        start();\r\n\r\n        let resizeTimer;\r\n        window.addEventListener(\'resize\', () => {\r\n            clearTimeout(resizeTimer);\r\n            resizeTimer = setTimeout(rebuild, 200);\r\n        });\r\n\r\n        // Pause when tab hidden (perf)\r\n        document.addEventListener(\'visibilitychange\', () => {\r\n            if (document.hidden) stop();\r\n            else if (!document.body.classList.contains(\'no-liquid\')) start();\r\n        });\r\n\r\n        // Re-init colors when theme changes\r\n        const themeObserver = new MutationObserver(() => { initBlobs(); });\r\n        themeObserver.observe(document.documentElement, { attributes: true, attributeFilter: [\'data-theme\'] });\r\n\r\n        // Expose controls for settings\r\n        window.liquidBg = {\r\n            enable() { document.body.classList.remove(\'no-liquid\'); start(); rebuild(); },\r\n            disable() { document.body.classList.add(\'no-liquid\'); stop(); ctx.clearRect(0,0,w,h); },\r\n            isOn() { return !document.body.classList.contains(\'no-liquid\'); }\r\n        };\r\n    })();\r\n    </script>\r\n\r\n    <!-- Settings Panel Script -->\r\n    <script>\r\n    (function() {\r\n        const SETTINGS_KEY = \'fedos_settings\';\r\n        const DEFAULT_SETTINGS = {\r\n            accent: \'default\',\r\n            density: \'comfortable\',\r\n            fontSize: 16,\r\n            liquidBg: true,\r\n            reducedMotion: false,\r\n            carouselAutoplay: true,\r\n            carouselSpeed: 5000,\r\n            showEta: true,\r\n            showMarquee: true,\r\n            showTagCloud: true,\r\n            defaultSort: \'hot\',\r\n            realtime: true,\r\n            sound: false,\r\n            desktopNotif: false,\r\n            kbdHint: true\r\n        };\r\n\r\n        let settings = Object.assign({}, DEFAULT_SETTINGS, JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(SETTINGS_KEY) || \'{}\'));\r\n\r\n        function saveSettings() { localStorage.setItem(SETTINGS_KEY, JSON.stringify(settings)); }\r\n        function save(key, val) { settings[key] = val; saveSettings(); }\r\n\r\n        // ---- Element refs ----\r\n        const settingsBtn = document.getElementById(\'settingsBtn\');\r\n        const settingsClose = document.getElementById(\'settingsClose\');\r\n        const settingsOverlay = document.getElementById(\'settingsOverlay\');\r\n        const navBtns = document.querySelectorAll(\'.settings-nav-btn\');\r\n        const sections = document.querySelectorAll(\'.settings-section\');\r\n\r\n        // ---- Open / close ----\r\n        function openSettings() {\r\n            settingsOverlay.classList.add(\'active\');\r\n            document.body.style.overflow = \'hidden\';\r\n            refreshDynamicLabels();\r\n        }\r\n        function closeSettings() {\r\n            settingsOverlay.classList.remove(\'active\');\r\n            document.body.style.overflow = \'\';\r\n        }\r\n        if (settingsBtn) settingsBtn.addEventListener(\'click\', openSettings);\r\n        if (settingsClose) settingsClose.addEventListener(\'click\', closeSettings);\r\n        if (settingsOverlay) settingsOverlay.addEventListener(\'click\', (e) => { if (e.target === settingsOverlay) closeSettings(); });\r\n        document.addEventListener(\'keydown\', (e) => { if (e.key === \'Escape\' && settingsOverlay.classList.contains(\'active\')) closeSettings(); });\r\n\r\n        // ---- Sidebar navigation ----\r\n        navBtns.forEach(btn => {\r\n            btn.addEventListener(\'click\', () => {\r\n                navBtns.forEach(b => b.classList.remove(\'active\'));\r\n                btn.classList.add(\'active\');\r\n                const target = btn.dataset.settingsSection;\r\n                sections.forEach(s => s.classList.toggle(\'active\', s.id === \'settings\' + target.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + target.slice(1)));\r\n            });\r\n        });\r\n\r\n        // ---- Apply functions ----\r\n        function applyAccent(accent) {\r\n            const root = document.documentElement;\r\n            if (!accent || accent === \'default\') {\r\n                root.style.removeProperty(\'--brand\');\r\n                root.style.removeProperty(\'--brand-2\');\r\n            } else {\r\n                root.style.setProperty(\'--brand\', accent);\r\n                root.style.setProperty(\'--brand-2\', accent);\r\n            }\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        function applyDensity(d) {\r\n            document.body.classList.toggle(\'density-compact\', d === \'compact\');\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        function applyFontSize(px) {\r\n            document.documentElement.style.setProperty(\'--font-base\', px + \'px\');\r\n            document.body.style.fontSize = px + \'px\';\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        function applyLiquidBg(on) {\r\n            if (window.liquidBg) { on ? window.liquidBg.enable() : window.liquidBg.disable(); userAvatar.textContent = name.charAt(0).toUpperCase();\r\n                loginBtn.title = \'Click to logout\';\r\n                // Show subscribers tab for logged-in users\r\n                if (subscribersTab) subscribersTab.style.display = \'\';\r\n                // Check admin status asynchronously\r\n                checkAdminStatus();\r\n            } else {\r\n                loginBtn.classList.remove(\'logged-in\');\r\n                loginBtnText.textContent = \'Login\';\r\n                userAvatar.textContent = \'👤\';\r\n                loginBtn.title = \'Click to login\';\r\n                // Hide subscribers tab for logged-out users\r\n                if (subscribersTab) subscribersTab.style.display = \'none\';\r\n                isAdmin = false;\r\n                // If currently on subscribers tab, switch to all\r\n                if (currentCategory === \'subscribers\') {\r\n                    currentCategory = \'all\';\r\n                    localStorage.setItem(\'fedos_category\', \'all\');\r\n                    filterByCategory(\'all\');\r\n                }\r\n            }\r\n            // Update channel pills based on login/admin state\r\n            updateChannelPillAvailability();\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        // Fetch the is_admin flag from the profiles table for the current user\r\n        async function checkAdminStatus() {\r\n            if (!currentUser) { isAdmin = false; updateChannelPillAvailability(); return; }\r\n            try {\r\n                const { data, error } = await supabaseClient.from(\'profiles\').select(\'is_admin\').eq(\'id\', currentUser.id).single();\r\n                if (error) { isAdmin = false; }\r\n                else { isAdmin = !!(data && data.is_admin); }\r\n            } catch (e) { isAdmin = false; }\r\n            updateChannelPillAvailability();\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        // Enable/disable channel pills in the new-topic form based on login & admin state\r\n        function updateChannelPillAvailability() {\r\n            if (!channelSelectWrap) return;\r\n            channelSelectWrap.querySelectorAll(\'.channel-pill\').forEach(pill => {\r\n                const ch = pill.dataset.channel;\r\n                // Notes channel requires admin\r\n                if (ch === \'notes\' && !isAdmin) {\r\n                    pill.classList.add(\'disabled\');\r\n                    pill.style.opacity = \'0.45\';\r\n                    pill.style.cursor = \'not-allowed\';\r\n                    pill.title = \'Only admins can post to Notes\';\r\n                } else {\r\n                    pill.classList.remove(\'disabled\');\r\n                    pill.style.opacity = \'\';\r\n                    pill.style.cursor = \'\';\r\n                    pill.title = \'\';\r\n                }\r\n            });\r\n        }\r\n        async function handleLogout() {\r\n            try { await supabaseClient.auth.signOut(); currentUser = null; updateUIForUser(null); resetAndLoad(); } catch (err) { console.error(\'Logout error:\', err); }\r\n        }\r\n        loginBtn.addEventListener(\'click\', function() { if (currentUser) { if (confirm(\'Logout?\')) handleLogout(); return; } openModal(); });\r\n        closeModal.addEventListener(\'click\', closeModalFunc);\r\n        loginModal.addEventListener(\'click\', function(e) { if (e.target === loginModal) closeModalFunc(); });\r\n        document.addEventListener(\'keydown\', function(e) { if (e.key === \'Escape\' && loginModal.classList.contains(\'active\')) closeModalFunc(); });\r\n\r\n        toggleAuthMode.addEventListener(\'click\', function() {\r\n            isSignUp = !isSignUp;\r\n            if (isSignUp) { modalTitle.textContent = \'Create Account\'; modalSub.textContent = \'Join loginEmail.value.trim();\r\n            if (!email) { showMessage(\'Please enter your email address first.\', \'error\'); return; }\r\n            try {\r\n                const { error } = await supabaseClient.auth.resetPasswordForEmail(email, { redirectTo: window.location.origin + window.location.pathname });\r\n                if (error) throw error;\r\n                showMessage(\'📧 Password reset email sent! Please sign in.\', \'error\'); return; }\r\n                if (!data.session) { showMessage(\'📧 Please check your email to confirm your account.\', \'info\'); }\r\n                else { showMessage(`✅ Account created! Welcome, ${email.split(\'@\')[0]}!`, \'success\'); currentUser = data.user; updateUIForUser(currentUser); setTimeout(closeModalFunc, 1000); resetAndLoad(); }\r\n            } catch (err) {\r\n                let msg = err.message;\r\n                if (err.message.includes(\'User already registered\')) msg = \'This email is already registered. Please sign in.\';\r\n                showMessage(msg, \'error\'); throw err;\r\n            }\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        loginForm.addEventListener(\'submit\', async function(e) {\r\n            e.preventDefault();\r\n            const email = loginEmail.value.trim();\r\n            const password = loginPassword.value;\r\n            if (!email || !password) { showMessage(\'Please fill in all fields.\', \'error\'); }\r\n        function updateBookmarkButtons() {\r\n            document.querySelectorAll(\'.bookmark-btn\').forEach(btn => {\r\n                const id = btn.dataset.topicId;\r\n                if (id && isBookmarked(id)) { btn.classList.add(\'bookmarked\'); if (error) throw error; sessionStorage.setItem(\'forum_viewed_session\', \'true\'); const el = document.getElementById(\'forumViewCount\'); if (el) el.textContent = data ?? 0; } catch (err) { console.error(\'Forum view increment failed:\', err); }\r\n        }\r\n        async function countTopicView(topicId) {\r\n            const key = `topic_viewed_${topicId}`;\r\n            if (sessionStorage.getItem(key)) return;\r\n            try { const { data, error } = await supabaseClient.rpc(\'increment_topic_views\', { p_topic_id: topicId }); if (error) throw error; sessionStorage.setItem(key, \'true\'); const viewEl = document.getElementById(\'topicViewCount\'); if (viewEl) viewEl.textContent = data ?? 0; updateTopicListViews(topicId, data ?? 0); } catch (err) { console.error(\'Topic view increment failed:\', err); }\r\n        }\r\n        function updateTopicListViews(topicId, newCount) {\r\n            const cards = document.querySelectorAll(\'.topic-card\');\r\n            for (const card of cards) { if (card.dataset.topicId == topicId) { const badge = card.querySelector(\'.views-badge\'); if (badge) badge.innerHTML = `<i class="fas fa-eye"></i> ${newCount}`; break; } }\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        // Sorting\r\n        function sortTopics(topics, sortBy) {\r\n            const now = Date.now(); const sorted = [...topics];\r\n            switch (sortBy) {\r\n                case \'latest\': sorted.sort((a, b) => new Date(b.created_at) - new Date(a.created_at)); break;\r\n                case \'top\': sorted.sort((a, b) => (b.like_count || 0) - (a.like_count || 0)); break;\r\n                case \'most_commented\': sorted.sort((a, b) => (b.replyCount || 0) - (a.replyCount || 0)); break;\r\n                case \'hot\': default: sorted.sort((a, b) => { const aAge = (now - new Date(a.created_at).getTime()) / (1000 * 60 * 60); const bAge = (now - new Date(b.created_at).getTime()) / (1000 * 60 * 60); const aScore = (a.like_count || 0) + (a.views || 0) / 10 - aAge / 4; const bScore = (b.like_count || 0) + (b.views || 0) / 10 - bAge / 4; return bScore - aScore; }); break;\r\n            }\r\n            return sorted;\r\n        }\r\n        function getReadingTime(text) { const words = text.trim().split(/\\\\s+/).length; const mins = Math.ceil(words / 200); return { words, mins }; }\r\n\r\n        function updateActiveUsers(topics) {\r\n            if (!topics || !topics.length) { activeUsersDisplay.textContent = \'\'; return; }\r\n            const users = new Set(); const oneDayAgo = Date.now() - 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;\r\n            topics.forEach(t => { if (new Date(t.created_at).getTime() > oneDayAgo) users.add(t.user_id); });\r\n            activeUsersDisplay.textContent = users.size > 0 ? `· 👥 ${users.size} active today` : \'\';\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        function buildTagCloud(topics) {\r\n            const tagCounts = {};\r\n            topics.forEach(t => { if (t.tags) t.tags.forEach(tag => tagCounts[tag] = (tagCounts[tag] || 0) + 1); });\r\n            const sorted = Object.entries(tagCounts).sort((a, b) => b[1] - a[1]).slice(0, 20);\r\n            if (sorted.length < 2) { tagCloud.classList.add(\'hidden\'); return; }\r\n            tagCloud.classList.remove(\'hidden\');\r\n            tagCloudContent.innerHTML = sorted.map(([tag, count]) => `<span class="tag-cloud-item" data-tag="${tag}">${tag} <span class="tag-count">(${count})</span></span>`).join(\'\');\r\n            document.querySelectorAll(\'.tag-cloud-item\').forEach(el => { el.addEventListener(\'click\', () => { searchInput.value = el.dataset.tag; filterTopics(el.dataset.tag); }); });\r\n        }\r\n\r\n        // Marquee\r\n        let postSnippets = [];\r\n        function loadSnippets() {\r\n            const stored = localStorage.getItem(\'fedos_post_snippets\');\r\n            if (stored) { try { postSnippets = JSON.parse(stored); } catch (e) { postSnippets = []; } }\r\n            if (postSnippets.length === 0 || (postSnippets.length === 1 && postSnippets[0].includes(\'Welcome\'))) {\r\n                if (allTopics && allTopics.length > 0) { seedMarqueeWithTopics(allTopics); saveSnippets(); }\r\n            }\r\n            updateMarquee();\r\n        }\r\n        function saveSnippets() { localStorage.setItem(\'fedos_post_snippets\', JSON.stringify(postSnippets)); }\r\n        function addSnippet(snippet) { if (postSnippets.includes(snippet)) return; postSnippets.unshift(snippet); if (postSnippets.length > 500) postSnippets = postSnippets.slice(0, 400); saveSnippets(); updateMarquee(); }\r\n        function seedMarqueeWithTopics(topics) {\r\n            if (!topics || topics.length === 0) return;\r\n            const hasRealTopic = postSnippets.some(s => s.includes(\' posted: "\'));\r\n            if (hasRealTopic) return;\r\n            const newSnippets = topics.slice(0, 10).map(t => { const name = t.profiles?.username || t.profiles?.email || \'Someone\'; return `${name} posted: "${t.title}"`; });\r\n            const filtered = newSnippets.filter(s => s && !s.includes(\'undefined\'));\r\n            if (filtered.length > 0) { postSnippets = filtered; postSnippets.push(\'🚀 Welcome to FEDPromptly!\'); saveSnippets(); updateMarquee(); }\r\n        }\r\n        function updateMarquee() {\r\n            if (postSnippets.length === 0) { globalMarquee.classList.add(\'hidden\');
